I've built the first part of the sync phase (S2a): one GM's campaigns now stay in sync across their own signed-in devices, encrypted end to end. It's committed on both branches but not pushed or deployed, so the live site doesn't have it yet.

**How it works for you:**
- **Turn on sync** under Account → Sync. You get a recovery key, shown once; keep it safe, because GMAtlas can't get it back for you. Then pick "Sync this campaign".
- **Another device:** sign in, and it asks to be approved and shows a 6-digit code. Approve it from a device that already syncs, under Account → Devices, after checking the code matches. Or type the recovery key. Then use "Open on this device".
- **Same campaign already synced from another device:** you choose "Use the synced copy" (the local copy goes to the backup slot first) or "Keep both".
- **While working:**
  - Edits show up on your other open devices within a fraction of a second.
  - A change made offline syncs when you reconnect.
  - A remote change waits while you're typing in a field and lands when you leave it.
- **Stop syncing** deletes the server copy; every device keeps its own.

**Privacy:** campaign content and titles are encrypted in the browser before they're sent. The server only stores ciphertext and wrapped keys, and the test confirmed no names or titles appear in its storage. Uploaded documents and images stay on the device they were added on. If you lose every device and the recovery key, the synced copy can't be recovered, but each device still has its full local copy.

**Testing:**
- 816 core and 19 server tests pass.
- In a check of two fake browsers running the real app against the real server code, all 23 steps passed with no console errors. They covered setup, approval, opening the campaign, live edits both ways, concurrent edits merging, the typing hold, a dropped connection, a reload, the ciphertext check, and stop syncing.
- A third-browser check of the recovery-key unlock passed, and so did a two-device test of the store's sync bookkeeping in isolation.
- Wrangler's dry run accepts the new server configuration.
- Not tested in a real browser yet.

**Not done yet in S2:**
- **What syncs:** Rules Profiles and Turn Step Lists don't sync yet. A campaign opened on a new device uses that device's first Rules Profile until this lands.
- **Visibility:** there's no sync status indicator on the campaign screen, no "who's online", and no "keep mine / take theirs" prompt when two devices edit the same field.
- **Limits:**
  - A single change over about 600 KB of text stays local and shows an error.
  - A device you sign out stays connected until its connection drops.
  - Signing a device out doesn't replace the account's encryption key.

The design decisions are in `design/adr/personal-sync.md`, and the server README and roadmap are updated.

**Deploying** adds a database change and a new type of server object, so I've left it for you to approve. It's `git push origin main server`, then `cd server; npm.cmd run migrate:remote; npm.cmd run deploy`. Want me to run it?

I've made all three changes, committed and pushed both branches, and deployed to the dev site only. Production at gmatlas.online is set up in config but not created or deployed.

1. **Recovery key by email.** When sync is set up, the key is emailed to the account's address and also shown once on screen. Account → Sync also has "Email me a new recovery key", which replaces the old key. The server passes the key through to the email and never stores it. **The catch:** this weakens the encryption guarantee. Sign-in codes go to the same inbox, so anyone who gets into that inbox can both sign in and unlock the synced campaigns. The server still holds nothing it can read. I recorded this trade-off in `design/adr/personal-sync.md`.
2. **Sync on by default.** While signed in:
   - The first device sets sync up without asking.
   - Any campaign you've edited starts syncing.
   - Campaigns from your other devices download, and a fresh device opens your most recent one.
   - A new device still needs one approval (from another device or the recovery key), and sign-in now lands on the Sync page to make that easy.
   - It leaves alone: untouched blank campaigns, campaigns you stopped syncing, and campaigns you deleted on that device.
   - Untouched blanks were the one surprise: on a fresh install the app quietly fills in default Party trackers, which made every blank campaign look edited. I fixed that on `main`.
3. **Logo menu.** Clicking the GMAtlas logo/title opens a menu with your name, email and sync state, plus Sign in or Account / Sync / Sign out, Settings, Help and About. The separate Settings button is gone, and the menu stays reachable when the header is squeezed. This also fixed an older bug where the "Sign in" label didn't update after you signed in.

**Dev vs production:**
- **Dev (dev.gmatlas.online):** `npm run deploy` in `server/` always goes here, from any branch.
- **Production (gmatlas.online):** fully separate Worker, database, sync storage, signing key and accounts. It only ships through `npm run release:production -- --confirm gmatlas.online`, which refuses unless:
  - you're on `main`, with no uncommitted changes, matching GitHub exactly;
  - all tests pass;
  - production is configured.

  It then tags the release (`prod-YYYYMMDD-HHMM`) and deploys. `npm run release:check` runs the checks without releasing.
- `CLAUDE.md` now says never to deploy to production unless you explicitly ask for a production release.

**Tests:**
- 816 core and 21 server tests pass.
- A three-browser run of the built app against the server code passed every check with no console errors, twice. It covered the new menu, automatic setup with the emailed key, a second device approved and getting the campaign, edits both ways, delete and stop-syncing staying put, a third device unlocking with the emailed key, and sign-out.
- The earlier sign-in, `file://` and store sync tests still pass.
- The release script refused correctly both from the `server` branch and without the confirmation.

**Live on dev:** the server confirms it's dev, signed-out requests are refused, and the new menu is in the app. Nothing answers at gmatlas.online. The first database migration attempt failed with a temporary Cloudflare authorisation error; the retry applied it fine.

**Before the first production release** (steps also in `server/README.md`):
1. Create the production database and paste its id into `wrangler.toml`.
2. Run `npm run secrets:production`, then add the new `p1` key to `src/data/certKeys.js`.
3. Set the Resend email key for production.
4. Merge `server` into `main` by pull request.

   - **Mid-turn addition:** "configure source code management to have the current deployment to a dev site at dev.gmatlas.online and then a production release at gmatlas.online. when deploying code as is being done now, only deploy that to dev instance until specific production release."
   - **Standing constraint:** deploy only to dev (dev.gmatlas.online). Production (gmatlas.online) only on an explicit, specific production release request. Now recorded in CLAUDE.md: "Never deploy to production unless the user explicitly asks for a production release."

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Platform and repo:** GMAtlas is a local-first PWA with a zero-dependency regex bundler (`scripts/build.js`). The bundler supports `export function`, `export async function`, `export const` and `export { }`; there's no `export class`, so classes go through the aggregate export.
   - **Branch rule:** core seams (`store.js`, domain, data, shell, CLAUDE.md) land on `main` first, then `git merge main` into `server`. Server, account, sync and portal work goes on `server`.
   - **Cloudflare:** Workers, D1, Durable Objects (SQLite-backed, WebSocket hibernation; a plain class, not `extends DurableObject`) and static assets. `wrangler.toml` has a default dev environment plus `[env.production]`.
   - **End-to-end encryption:**
     - device ECDH P-256 key pair, private half non-extractable, stored in IndexedDB;
     - vault key (32 bytes) wrapped per device (ECDH-ES+HKDF+AES-GCM) and to a recovery key (Crockford base32, 20 bytes, 8 groups of 4);
     - per-campaign keys sealed with the vault key;
     - envelopes bound to campaign id and purpose;
     - 6-digit approval code derived from the device's public key.
   - **Store sync:** `sync:<id>` holds `{v, deviceId, base, meta, hlc, cursor, outbox, nextBatch}`. Changes are found by diffing base against the live doc, debounced 100 ms. `DocMap` subclass of Map. The doc and sync state are written in one transaction (`idbPutMany`); `withoutInlineBytes` strips `data:` URLs before diffing.
   - **Room protocol:** hello/welcome/ops/caughtUp/push/ack/snapshotReq/reset/revoked/error.
     - 60-second socket tickets (`typ:'ws'`) that can't pass as access tokens.
     - Catch-up frames of at most 512 KB; snapshots over HTTP in 1 MB chunks.
     - The room asks for a snapshot every 1000 ops (`COMPACT_EVERY`); ops older than 30 days are trimmed.
   - **Sync by default (`autoSync`):**
     - the vault is set up automatically and the recovery key emailed;
     - edited campaigns sync; remote campaigns are downloaded; a fresh device switches to the newest one;
     - exceptions: the `edited` flag (false for blanks), `syncOptOut`, `syncDeclined`, and collisions (left for the GM's prompt).
   - **Store `edited` flag:** `noteEdited` diffs synced paths, ignoring `meta.updatedAt` and local paths. `update(fn, {system:true})` marks app housekeeping, which doesn't count.
   - **Environments:** dev (`gmatlas`, `db_gmatlas`, key k1) and production (`gmatlas-production`, `db_gmatlas_production`, key p1). The release script guards production; `/api/health` reports the environment.

3. Files and Code Sections (most recent session focus):

- **A device joining for the first time:** it takes the account's settings and adds anything only it has. **What the admin page does:**
- **Users:** search by email, name or id, and see an account's devices, sessions, licenses and sync setup. You can disable an account (which signs it out everywhere at once), sign out one device or all, and set admin roles. Campaign titles and contents never appear, because they're encrypted end to end.
- **Entitlements:** grant a book license, a feature or a campaign module, permanently or as a trial with an end date. You can also change an end date, revoke, or turn a self-ticked "I own this book" into a confirmed grant. Bulk grant takes CSV: it checks every line first and grants all or nothing, and "Check" only validates. Nothing can be granted that isn't in the app's own catalogs.
- **Feature flags:** for each feature you can set a default, a kill switch that turns it off for everyone, per-account overrides by email, and a percentage rollout. The catalog has three features: sync (on), device-to-device transfer and multiplayer tables (both off until those phases exist).
- **Audit log:** every change, with who made it, before and after, and the note. Every change requires a note.
- **Roles:**
  - owner: everything, and the only role that can change roles;
  - admin: everything except roles;
  - support: users and grants, and can view flags but not change them;
  - content: flags only.

  The server checks the role on every request, and there's always at least one owner.
- **Separate from the app:** the admin page is its own download, so none of its code reaches ordinary users. It only exists on the service sites, not the GitHub Pages copy.

**This also finished S2:**
- **Sync switch:** sync is now a feature the admin page can turn off. When it's off, the server refuses sync writes, and the app shows "Sync is turned off for this account" without touching local data.
- **Update delay:** a grant, revoke or flag change reaches an open app within about 10 minutes, or as soon as the tab is reopened.

**Tests:**
- 829 core and 27 server tests pass, including 5 new admin test groups.
- A new end-to-end test drove the real admin page and checked, with no console errors:
  - the role gate;
  - search and user detail;
  - a grant reaching the user's app;
  - the kill switch turning sync off in their app and back on;
  - the audit trail and disabling an account;
  - the support role seeing flags read-only.
- All earlier end-to-end tests still pass.

**Fixed along the way:** a failed admin action, such as a missing note, used to wipe the form you'd filled in, and that's what made my first grant test pick the wrong license. Forms now keep what you typed. A bulk grant with bad lines now shows the per-line errors rather than a generic message.

**Production:** the next release applies the two new database migrations automatically. Afterwards, make yourself owner there with `npm run admin:role -- --email meta5by5@gmail.com --role owner --env production` in `server/`, after signing in once on gmatlas.online.

- moving third-party PDF loading to "import my copy";
- a help article on accounts.

**Things you need to look at:**

2. **Starsmith.** The two Starsmith PDFs (Eric Bright's Starforged supplement) are filed under the open Starforged license, so the online site now serves one of them. I couldn't confirm Starsmith is CC-BY. If it isn't, it should get its own license like the Monster Manual.
3. **Google and Discord sign-in** need a one-time app registration per site:
   - **Google:** Cloud Console › Credentials › OAuth client.
   - **Discord:** Developer Portal › OAuth2.
   - **Redirect address:** `https://dev.gmatlas.online/api/auth/oauth/google/callback` (or `…/discord/callback`).
   - Then set the client id and secret with `npx wrangler secret put GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` and `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` in `server/` (or the `DISCORD_` pair). Full steps are in `server/README.md`.

**What's new:**
- **Per-network limit:** at most 20 sign-in codes and 30 wrong codes per network per hour, on top of the per-email limits. Addresses are stored only as scrambled hashes.
- **Passkeys:** sign in with fingerprint, face or device PIN, and no email. You add one under **Account › Sign-in**, and Profile and Sync offer "Sign in faster next time" until you do.
  - The server verifies every passkey itself and refuses wrong-site use, replays and cloned keys.
  - Each site has its own passkeys, so a dev passkey won't work on production.
- **Continue with Google / Discord:**
  - The first time, it joins your existing account if the provider has verified the same email address.
  - **Account › Sign-in** connects or disconnects them.
  - Your email code always keeps working as the fallback.
  - Apple isn't included: it sends its answer back in a way this server refuses by design, so it's a separate piece of work.
- **Import my copy:**
  - **Other publishers' books:** on the online site, they now offer **⬆ Import my copy** (or **Import my copies** for several at once) instead of Load. The site never serves them.
  - **Open books:** they keep **Load**, plus Import.
  - **Too-large open PDFs:** the Ironsworn and Starforged rulebooks and the Starsmith Assets PDF are each over Cloudflare's 25 MB per-file limit, so they aren't hosted. Use Import for those.
  - **Your own machine:** running locally, Load still reads your own `assets/docs/` folder.

I merged `server` into `main` and built S3, which lets a file added on one of your devices reach the others. The merge and the first half of S3 are pushed to `main`; the device-to-device half is on `server` and live on dev.gmatlas.online. gmatlas.online is unchanged.

**Merge:** `main` now has S1 and S2. `gh` isn't installed here, so I merged locally (no pull request), as last time, and ran both test suites before pushing.

**S3, part 1 – documents keyed by their contents** (on `main`, so GitHub Pages has it too):
- **Content keys:** uploaded documents are now stored under a fingerprint of their contents, so the same file has the same key on every device. Older uploads are converted in the background on load; the old key keeps working, and their Table of Contents data moves with them.
- **"Isn't on this device yet":** a synced document whose file was added on another device shows ☁̸ and a count in the Docs library. Opening it shows a card with **Import file…**.
- **Import matches by contents:** a file a document is missing fills it in even if it was renamed, and importing a file that's already there no longer makes a duplicate.
- **Exports:** library export zips record each file's fingerprint, and imports skip a damaged file instead of storing it.

**S3, part 2 – sending files between your devices** (on `server`, deployed to dev):
- **Where:** **Get it from <device>** appears on that card, in the Docs library, and in the logo menu (which also covers gallery images).
- **How it travels:** the file goes straight from one device to the other over WebRTC (direct browser-to-browser) and never touches the server. The connection setup passes through the server, but it's encrypted with the campaign key, so the server only sees which device each message is for. Each file is checked against its fingerprint before it's stored.
- **What a device will send:** only files the open campaign uses, and only to your own devices.
- **Direct connections only:** a new **Account › Sync** section explains the transfer and has this per-device setting, which keeps relays out at both ends.
- **One change from the plan:** setup goes through the campaign's sync channel rather than the account-wide one the plan named, since that doesn't exist yet. It's recorded in the new design note, `design/adr/document-transfer.md`.

**To try it on dev:** the feature is off by default. In the admin page, under Feature flags, turn on **Device-to-device document transfer** for your account. Then open the same synced campaign on two devices.

**Relay server (optional):** without one, transfers work only where the two devices can reach each other directly; some office, school and mobile networks block that. To add a relay, set `TURN_KEY_ID` and `TURN_KEY_API_TOKEN` from Cloudflare Realtime › TURN; `server/README.md` has the steps.

**Tests:**
- 845 core and 35 server tests pass, and all 11 end-to-end suites pass, including two new ones.
- The new two-browser run uses the real server code with a stand-in for WebRTC, since Node has none:
  - a corrupted transfer is refused and nothing is stored;
  - a 200 KB PDF arrives byte for byte;
  - the server only ever saw encrypted setup messages;
  - with Direct connections only, no relay reached either device.
- Real WebRTC between two actual browsers hasn't been tried yet; that's the dev test above.

**Not done yet:**
- Sourcebook PDFs don't transfer device to device yet: they're stored by file path rather than contents, and the receiving device needs the license too.
- Both devices need the campaign open at the same time.

**To see it:** reload the app on each device. An already-open tab can take up to about 10 minutes to pick up the change, or until you switch back to it. After the reload, **Account › Sync** shows the new **Files between your devices** section.

**To try a transfer:**

No relay server is set up, so the two devices have to be able to reach each other directly. If a transfer doesn't connect between, say, a phone on mobile data and a desktop, that's the likely reason. Adding Cloudflare's TURN secrets would fix it.

To turn it off again: admin page › Feature flags › Device-to-device document transfer, remove your override.

I've fixed it, and the fix is live on dev.gmatlas.online.

**What was wrong:** the Docs panel redrew itself while the file picker was open. On a phone, opening the picker hides the page, and coming back makes sync, the sign-in refresh and the device-transfer checks all update, which triggers a redraw. The file you picked went to the old, replaced button, so the app never received it. It could hit any file picker in the app, not just Docs.

**The fix:** the button you opened the picker from is now put back after a redraw, so the file you pick always gets through. The change is in [shell.js](src/ui/shell.js).

**Checked:** I wrote a test that opens the picker, forces a redraw, then picks a file. It fails without the fix (the file is lost) and passes with it, for both "Import files…" and "📥 Import File(s)". All 845 tests and the other end-to-end tests pass. Dev is serving the fixed version, v0.100.1.

**To try it:** reload the app on your phone first, so it picks up the new version.

"Import files…" in the "not on this device" note only fills documents that are missing that exact file. If you pick a different copy of the file (a re-download can differ by a few bytes), it says "matched nothing missing" and doesn't add it. To add any file as a new document, use "📥 Import File(s)" instead.

The fix is committed on `main` (04c054f) and merged into `server`. I haven't pushed either branch, so GitHub Pages doesn't have it yet. Production is untouched.

A device only sends books whose license it owns, and only to your own devices. The receiver checks each file against a hash the sender provides and refuses anything that doesn't match. In the test, a tampered book was refused and real ones arrived intact.

**2. Continue with Apple** (a9c380c). It's built and tested against a fake Apple, but it stays hidden until you set four Apple secrets. That needs a paid Apple Developer membership; the steps are in `server/README.md`. Apple sends people back with a post from its own site, so the service now lets exactly that one request through. Apple users with "Hide My Email" get a new account unless they first connect Apple from their existing account. The help page explains this.

**3. Device-bound sessions** (fad953b). Each browser now holds a signing key that can't be copied out of it, and its session is tied to that key. A stolen sign-in cookie used from another computer gets nothing. Existing sessions, including yours, tie themselves to the browser on their next refresh, so nobody gets signed out. Your browser's clock being wrong doesn't break it.

Two side effects:
- The admin page now signs its requests with the app's key, which pulls more code into its file: it grows from about 0.1 MB to 0.9 MB. Only admins load it.
- This added a database migration. I applied it to dev before deploying; production will need it applied the same way before its next release.

**4. TURN relay — needs you:**
1. In the Cloudflare dashboard, go to **Realtime › TURN Server › Create** and copy the **key ID** and **API token**. TURN has a free monthly allowance.
2. From `server/`, run `npx wrangler secret put TURN_KEY_ID` and `npx wrangler secret put TURN_KEY_API_TOKEN`, pasting the values when asked. That way the secrets don't pass through this chat.

After that, device-to-device transfers work even when the two devices can't connect directly, for example a phone on mobile data. The "Direct connections only" option still turns the relay off per device.

When you want S4 (store and billing), I'll build it provider-neutral, as you chose: catalog, cart, orders and redeem codes behind an adapter with a fake test provider.

Checking where the wiki is already referenced. Next I'll write the roadmap.

Recon is done. The roadmap is core-app work, so I'm writing it on `main`.

Roadmap written. Now pointing the triage list and CLAUDE.md at it.

I wrote the roadmap at [design/WIKI-ROADMAP.md](design/WIKI-ROADMAP.md) but haven't built any of the wiki yet. Your file asks for a roadmap, and the prompt inside it says to check the code and report before writing any, so I've stopped at that review point (phase W0). It's on `main` and not committed. I also updated [design/feature-triage.yml](design/feature-triage.yml) to point at it, and added it to the design-doc list in CLAUDE.md.

**What already exists.** A lot of the brief is already in GMAtlas:
- entities linked in one graph;
- `@[Name]` links with autocomplete;
- places nested inside places;
- hexes linked to entities;
- dungeon maps with floors and rooms;
- the journal, search and the graph view.

The biggest gaps:
- nothing shows what links to a page (backlinks);
- renaming an entity breaks every `@[Old Name]` link to it;
- you can't mark text as GM-only, and there's no Player View;
- dungeon rooms can't be linked to an entity;
- there's no building or floor structure;
- the journal has no session numbers or in-world dates;
- there's no markdown export.

**Where I departed from the brief**, because it assumes things that would break the app's design rules:
- **No new storage layer.** The brief's separate storage adapter and its own entity and link tables would break the one-campaign-document rule. Sync to Cloudflare already exists, so the wiki reads and writes the campaign as everything else does.
- **Search stays in the browser.** Synced text is encrypted, so Cloudflare's database can't search it. MiniSearch would add a runtime dependency, so I extend the app's own search instead.
- **Drive backups run only while the app is open.** The brief stores Google tokens on the Worker so it can back up with every tab closed. The Worker can't read the encrypted campaign, and document files never pass through our server, so each device uploads straight to your Drive. Nothing backs up while every device is closed.

**Please confirm these four choices before I change the data format:**
1. **Dungeons, rooms, buildings and floors become Locations** with a "place kind" field, not new entity types. They keep every existing location feature.
2. **`[[Name]]` and `@[Name]` mean the same link.** Both can be typed. The label order differs: `[[Name|shown text]]` versus `@[shown text|Name]`.
3. **Renaming rewrites every link and keeps the old name as an alias,** so text typed or imported later with the old name still finds the page. Links stay name-based because switching them to IDs would rewrite every stored text.
4. **Player View hides GM content.** It hides GM-only text, `:::gm` blocks and entities marked GM-only; entities start as GM-only. One function produces every player-facing copy: the Player View, markdown export, Drive shares, and later shared player tables.

If those four hold, I'll start W1 (the data additions, link parsing, backlinks and rename, with tests), then stop again for review.

What's in place:
- **`[[ ]]` links.** `[[Name]]` and `[[Name|shown text]]` work alongside `@[…]` and mean the same thing. A name is looked up by entity name first, then its aliases, then documents, then maps.
- **Aliases count everywhere.** Every existing `@mention` lookup now also checks an entity's aliases.
- **Backlinks.** For any entity, the app can work out everything that links to it, and where each link is. It's worked out from the text each time and never saved, so it can't go stale. It looks in:
  - entity fields;
  - the journal;
  - scenes and the Composer;
  - threads and the Guide;
  - text documents;
  - hex, dungeon-tile and battlemap notes.
- **Red links.** It can list every link name that doesn't match anything.
- **Rename.** It keeps each link's shown text and page number. If another entity also has the old name, no links are rewritten, because they now mean that other entity.
- **New optional fields** on entities:
  - aliases;
  - who can see the entity's page (GM-only unless shared with players);
  - for Locations, a place kind: region, hex, site, building, floor, dungeon, level, room or other.
- **Two new relationship types.** Connects To is for doors, passages and stairs, and mirrors itself on the other end. Appears In links an entity to an Encounter.
- **Page data.** Everything a page will show is assembled in one place:
  - an infobox and a breadcrumb up the location tree;
  - the places inside it, and who or what is located there;
  - its relationships, grouped by type;
  - what links to it, and which journal entries mention it.

**Not built yet:** the wiki page view, and any controls for aliases, visibility or place kind (W2). Linking a dungeon room to a Location comes in W3. Until W2, a typed `[[Name]]` shows as plain text in the editors, though it already counts as a link.

The new code is in [src/domain/wiki.js](src/domain/wiki.js). I also updated the roadmap, the triage note, CLAUDE.md, the Constitution, and the version (now 0.99.3).

**How to use it:**
- **Wiki panel (📜 in the edge nav).**
  - It lists every page, grouped by type.
  - You can search names, aliases and tags, and filter by type.
  - 👁 marks pages shared with players, and ↩ shows how many places link to each page.
  - A Red links list shows names you've linked to that have no page yet.
- **Read and Edit.** Every entity tab now has 📖 Read and ✎ Edit. Read shows the entity as a page:
  - title, type and place kind, and other names it goes by;
  - the overview, with working `[[ ]]` and `@` links;
  - a facts box, and the trail of places it sits inside;
  - what it contains, who and what is there, and its relationships;
  - **Linked from**, grouped by where the link is; journal, Guide and map entries open when clicked;
  - **Appears in sessions**, listing journal entries that mention it.

  Clicking a link on a page opens the next page in Read mode too.
- **Red links.** Clicking a link with no page shows a row of type buttons, and the page is created in one click.
- **GM-only text.** Put it between a line `:::gm` and a line `:::`. It shows as a shaded GM box, and the "Revealed / hidden (GM)" notes show the same way.
- **Page settings,** at the foot of each page: other names (aliases), who can see the page, and place kind.
- **Player View.** One 👁 switch covers the panel and every page. GM-only pages show as hidden, and shared pages show only:
  - the overview without GM blocks;
  - links only to other shared pages;
  - a few public facts;
  - shared neighbouring places and relations.

  It never shows GM notes, goals, aliases, relationship notes or backlinks. A test checks this output for GM text.

**One correction to what you approved:** I had decision 4 (Player View) backwards. The Entity Editor labels `overview` "What the party knows" and `revealed` "Revealed / hidden (GM)", so players see the overview and never the revealed notes. I built it that way and corrected the roadmap.

     - no new storage;

## Response (2026-11-09 18:54:34 UTC)

VitePress would mean adding Vue, Vite and a Node build step that turns Markdown files into a site. GMAtlas wiki content lives in the browser (IndexedDB, encrypted when synced) and has no build step or dependencies, so VitePress would be the very different toolset you ruled out. I'll enhance the current wiki instead. Let me look at the page and entity shapes first.

Now the entity record shape per type, so the page can show every field.

Encounters are already Encounter-type entities, so they'll get the same full-record page. Next I'm writing the field catalog (as data) and the domain function that reads it.

Now the player-safe copy gets the whitelisted record fields:

Now the page view: an attractive record layout with `{none}` for unset fields, and an outline the portal's left nav can use.

Now the route for a section (`#/wiki/section/<type>`):

Now the portal's wiki views get a left nav with every section, plus "On this page" when a page is open:

Widening the portal's main column for the wiki, then adding styles:

Styles are done; next I'm adding tests for the record and section route, then building.

Statblocks also carry weapons, items and attacks, so I'll check their shape to include them:

All 870 tests pass (5 new). Now a jsdom smoke run against the built bundle for the portal nav and record layout.

The only failure is jsdom lacking `CSS.escape`, which every browser has and the Help page already uses. I'll shim it in the harness and run the new smoke alongside the earlier wiki and app smokes:

All smoke suites pass. Now the docs: build info, the roadmap and CLAUDE.md counts.

I kept the current wiki and added both features to it rather than moving to VitePress, because VitePress would mean a very different toolset. It builds a site from Markdown files using Vue, Vite and a Node build step, but your wiki content lives in each browser's campaign data (encrypted when synced), so there's nothing for that build to read. I built its useful parts here instead: the left sidebar, the per-page "On this page" list and the fixed record layout.

All 870 tests pass, including 5 new ones. A simulated-browser run of the new features passed with no console errors, and the earlier test runs for the wiki and the main app still pass. Nothing is committed and dev hasn't been redeployed yet.

**Left nav on the wiki page**
- **Sections:** All pages, one section per entity type (NPC, Location, Faction, Asset, Lore, Item, Encounter, Lifeform) with a page count, and Red links (GM only). Each section has its own address, like `#/wiki/section/npc`, and search stays within the open section.
- **On an entity page:** the nav also gets an "On this page" list of the page's sections and each card in its Details section. Clicking one scrolls to it, and the breadcrumb now includes the section.
- **On a phone** the sections become a horizontal strip of buttons above the page.

**Entity pages show the whole record**
- **Every field shows:** each page lays out the record as cards. Basics (tags, also-known-as, who can see it) comes first, then the type's own groups; for a location, those are Place, Story and senses, and World profile.
- **Unset fields** show `{none}`, and so do an empty Overview and empty GM notes.
- **Statblocks** each get their own card, including weapons, items and attacks.
- **Nothing is left out:** any field a record carries that isn't in my list appears under "Other fields".
- **Links:** fields that point to another entity, such as "Takes place at", are clickable.
- **Encounters** are already Encounter-type entities, so they get the same treatment, including session hooks and their other records.
- **Player View** shows the same cards with only the player-safe fields.
- **Inside the campaign**, the 📖 Read view uses the same layout.

**Two things you might not expect:**
- **Genre-specific groups:** the fantasy settlement fields only show in fantasy campaigns, and the sci-fi World profile only outside them. Either still appears if it holds data. Without this, every location would show nine or more `{none}` rows that don't apply.
- **Changing what a page shows:** which fields each type shows, and their labels, live in a data file, [src/data/wikiRecordFields.js](src/data/wikiRecordFields.js). Edit that file to add, rename or reorder fields; no page code changes.

I also updated the build notes (v0.99.6), the wiki roadmap (including why VitePress wasn't adopted), and the counts in `CLAUDE.md`.

Should I commit this on `main`, merge it into `server` and deploy to dev? The earlier commits on both branches are still unpushed.

Picking one fills the row's name, range, shots, damage and traits, and the ＋ disappears. | Pack | Items | What's in it |
|---|---|---|
| Five Parsecs from Home | 114 | All 36 weapons, plus gun mods, sights, consumables, protective devices, implants, utility devices and on-board items |
| Five Parsecs Compendium | 61 | Dramatic Weapons table, Bug Hunt weapons and trooper armor, psionic equipment, bot upgrades |
| Planetfall | 54 | Standard, Tier 1 and Tier 2 weapons, grunt upgrades, all artifacts |
| Five Leagues | 123 | Weapons, armor, helmet and shield, assorted items, and every enchanted item (weapons, armor, potions, jewelry, clothing, apparatus, unique items) |

**Tags:** weapons are `#weapon` plus `#melee` or `#ranged`. Everything else is `#gear` plus its list's tag, such as `#implant`, `#gun-mod`, `#potion` or `#rune`.

**Each item** has a gear statblock with the book's numbers. Its effect is a short summary in my own words rather than the book's text, ending with the book and page it came from. Five Leagues needed a new gear statblock for this.

**Gating** uses the same rule as the existing lifeform packs. The packs appear under Settings › Campaigns › Source Data only for books in use in the campaign; owning a book isn't enough. A Compendium counts as in use once it's picked for one of the rules areas in Settings. I also closed a gap: the Licenses tab used to offer Import for any owned book. It now follows the same rule, and the import itself refuses a book that isn't in use.

**Duplicate names:** import skips any item whose name the campaign already has. Variants that reuse a core name are therefore named by source, such as "Hand cannon (Dramatic)", "Hand cannon (Bug Hunt)", "Colony rifle (Planetfall)" and "Staff (Deep Below)". You can import every pack into one campaign and lose nothing.

Imported weapons show up in the Five Parsecs weapon-row ＋ picker.

**Left out on purpose:** starship parts and advanced training, which aren't personal equipment. Also Deep Below's magic weapons and gear, because the book generates those from roll tables rather than listing named items.

**Decision for you:** like the existing lifeform packs, these files sit on the hosted site. Gating only controls what the app offers, so anyone who knows a file's address can download it. That was already true for the enemy packs, but this is a lot more book content. If that matters before deploying, they could follow the Shadowdark approach of being built from the user's own PDF instead.

The packs are written by [scripts/build-equipment-packs.js](scripts/build-equipment-packs.js), so corrections go there and you rerun it.

Everything you asked for this round is built and working. It's all uncommitted on `main` as v0.101.0. All 883 tests pass (8 new), and simulated-browser runs of each change pass with no console errors, as do the earlier ones.

**Composer and Encounter wizard**
- **Tabs by default:** the Composer now has the wizard's WHO / WHERE / WHAT / WHY / HOW tabs and shows one section at a time. A filled section's tab gets a ✓, and Activity is on the HOW tab.
- **Guided wizard:** 🪄✨ adds a guide above the section at each step:
  - a few questions for the step (for example, "Who stands in the way? Give them a reason the party could understand.");
  - suggested oracle tables for the step, which roll in place. WHAT's suggestions follow the Intent you've set.
- **What you can do with a roll:**
  - ✔ Use adds the result to that step's field;
  - at WHO, ＋ NPC turns it into an NPC and asks for their role in the scene;
  - 📖 sends it to the Journal, and 🔀 Others shows more tables.
- **Navigation:** Back / Next move between steps, and the last step offers Save as Encounter. Leaving the wizard keeps you on the same tab.
- The add-NPC and add-faction buttons are now just 🧑 and 🏛.

**Profiles and statblocks**
- **Five Parsecs:** the "5PFH" profile is now "Five Parsecs". Its Module for Profile is Five Parsecs from Home and its Party Roster headline fields are Speed, Combat, Tough. On an existing install the profile is renamed once. The module and headline fields are only changed if they're still the original defaults, so anything you've set yourself stays.
- **Five Leagues:** "Five Leagues (Storyboard)" is now "Five Leagues from the Borderlands".
- **Toughness → Tough:** the three statblock templates you named now say Tough. Existing NPCs, any edited copies of those templates, and the three lifeform packs are updated to match, keeping their values. Five Leagues keeps Toughness.

**Source Data Remove**
- Once a pack is in the campaign, its Import button becomes Remove. Remove asks first, then deletes every record the pack added, along with any links to them.
- A pack stays listed, marked "not in use", after its book is dropped, so you can still remove it. Without this, it would disappear exactly when you want it gone.
- Records imported before today become removable once you press Import on that pack again. That re-import also claims any of your own records with the same name and type, so Remove would delete those too.
- Removing a Hostile zone takes only its own zone, stars and worlds, not the bases every zone shares.

**Party panel and Navigator**
- The Starship section moved from the Party tab to the Starship tab, under the ship's picture and name, open by default. The Starship tab only appears in campaigns using Five Parsecs, so the section no longer shows in other sci-fi campaigns such as Traveller.
- "Generate Mission" reads "Generate Quest" in fantasy campaigns, and its Journal note and message say Quest.
- "Advance Faction Turns" is hidden when the profile has Faction Events turned off.

**In the Composer**
- **Which Encounter:** a name field at the top says *New Encounter* or *Editing Encounter*.
  - 💾 Save Encounter updates that record.
  - The first save (＋ Save as Encounter) creates the record, and the Composer then keeps editing it.
  - ＋ New starts another Encounter; 📂 opens the record.
- **The Encounter's other fields** now sit in the tab they fit, each with a 🎲 that rolls a suitable oracle into it:

  | Tab | New fields |
  |---|---|
  | WHO | Someone innocent gets hurt |
  | WHAT | Status, What people say it's about, What lit the fuse |
  | WHY | What's actually driving it, Why the gap matters, Deep root, Party leverage |
  | HOW | Session hooks, Last de-escalation attempt, GM notes |

- **Wizard:** a rolled result now has a menu of the step's fields, the new ones included, so ✔ Use can send it to any of them.
- **Factions:** WHO's factions list is now the Encounter's "Factions involved". ＋ adds any faction to the encounter and ✕ leaves one out.

**On the Encounter record**
- Every Composer field is saved on it: the cast, assets, places and factions, plus site, surroundings, situation, intent, threat and mystery, objective, mode and activity.
- A new "Who, where, what, why, how" section shows them without the oracles or tips. Text fields edit in place; the lists change through the Composer.
- "Involved" is now the same faction thumbnails with ＋ / ✕, replacing the dropdown.
- **📝 Open in Composer** loads the record back into the Composer. It asks first if the Composer holds something else. Encounters saved before today load from their existing links.

**Behaviour changes you might notice**
- **Loading replaces the current scene:** it puts the Encounter's cast, places and WHO…HOW into the current scene, replacing what was there.
- **Factions ＋:** it used to permanently link a faction to the location. Now it only adds the faction to this encounter.
- **Contested-zone picker gone:** the "Location (contested zone)" picker under WHO is removed. An Encounter's location is now the first place in WHERE.
- **Save no longer opens the record;** use 📂 for that.
- **Not in the Composer:** irreversible facts, faction postures and information asymmetry stay on the record only, since they're structured rather than single text fields.

**How it works:**
- **⤢ Maximize** sits in the panel's top bar. It also appears when the map is anchored to the Storyboard as its own card. It's desktop only, because phones and tablets don't show the Storyboard's three sections.
- **Maximizing** widens the map into one card covering the left and center sections. It's taken out of the tab strip so it isn't shown twice. Any other panel anchored to the Storyboard goes back to the tab strip.
- **The right section shows Party:**
  - If the profile's right position is Party, all open tabs close so that position shows Party.
  - Otherwise, Party opens as the active tab on the right, and your other tabs stay open.
- **⤡ Restore** on the maximized card puts the map back in the panel as the active tab.

- On the web, a signed-out startup screen now asks you to sign in before showing campaigns.
  - **Sign in** opens the normal sign-in page and comes back to the campaign list when you're done.
  - **Play offline** lists the campaigns kept only on this device. Account campaigns need signing in, there and in the header's campaign switcher.
- Opening the app from the file directly works as before, since signing in isn't possible there.
- I couldn't prove why you were being signed out. Dev's sessions show the server never ended any of yours. But each sign-in created a new device record, which means the browser had lost the app's stored data. I made three fixes:
  - the app now asks the browser to keep its storage permanently;
  - a browser that kept its sign-in cookie but lost the saved user now signs back in quietly;
  - I fixed a first-launch race that could lose the key each session depends on, which signs you out at the next visit.
- If your browser is set to clear site data when it closes, you'll still be signed out each time. That's a browser setting the app can't override.

     - Lossless migration.

     - store.get() overlays `profile.ruleset` onto settings.
     - store.update now gives mutators the overlaid ruleset and restores untouched keys.
   - **Encounter scoping.**
     - `[data-encounter-form="<id>"]` sets `encounterScope`; store.update is hooked so that `applyToEncounter` handles writes.

**How text is split:**

- **No bold on titles:** Metamorphous has no bold weight, and fake bold smears it, so titles stand out by size and colour.
- **Name field:** the Encounter name field was showing Arial, so it now follows the theme.

Lancelot's capitals still look odd in body text that's all-caps, like "WHO/WHERE" inside a sentence. Tell me if that bothers you.

Signing in now comes before any campaign list, and each campaign belongs either to an account or to the free version. It's committed on `server` and live on dev (v0.113.3), not pushed. The fantasy font work is also committed on `main` and merged into `server`, so it's on dev too.

**The startup screen, on the web, every time the app opens**
- **If this browser is still signed in:** "Continue as Sam", "Sign in as a different user" and "Free version".
- **If signed out:** "Sign in or create an account" and "Free version".
- **No campaigns show until you choose.** After that, a "Change" link takes you back to the choice.
- **"Sign in as a different user"** signs out first, then opens sign-in. Once you're signed in, it goes straight to that account's campaigns without asking again. If you sign in with Google or Discord, you'll see the "Continue as…" choice once more.
- **Opened from the file:** nothing changes, since accounts aren't available there.

**How campaigns are tied**
- **Ownership:** a campaign belongs to whatever you were using when you created it, or to the account it was synced to or downloaded from.
- **Lists:** the startup list and the header's campaign switcher show only the chosen side's campaigns. Two people sharing one browser each see only their own.
- **Older campaigns:** ones that were already syncing count as the signed-in account's.

**Decision for you:** a campaign made before signing in now stays in the free version; it no longer syncs into your account automatically. Moving it in takes one click: **Sync** next to it on Account › Sync. I chose this so an account never picks up free campaigns without you asking, but it reverses part of the "sync by default" behaviour. The other way is to bring free campaigns into the account automatically the first time you sign in; say if you'd prefer that.

**Testing**
- All 900 unit tests and the 43 server tests pass, and every simulated-browser suite passes.
- New checks cover:
  - the signed-in choices on reload;
  - the free version hiding the account's campaign;
  - signing in as a brand-new user on a browser that holds another user's campaign, which correctly doesn't list it.
- Six suites assumed campaigns made before signing in would sync by themselves; I updated them to the new rule.

The help article on syncing explains the new choice, and the reasoning is in [campaign-ownership.md](design/adr/campaign-ownership.md). The rules themselves are in [campaignOwnership.js](src/domain/campaignOwnership.js).

`main` doesn't have its Settings → Build note for the font changes yet; that note is only on `server`. I can add it to `main` in a small follow-up commit.

## Response (2026-11-21 02:34:51 UTC)

I'll start by reviewing the faction code that already exists, so the plan builds on it.

I have enough context on the existing faction code. Checking how the existing roadmaps are formatted so this one matches.

Writing the plan to `design/FACTION-ROADMAP.md`, alongside the other roadmaps.

Adding a pointer to the new roadmap in CLAUDE.md, then committing.

Written for: you as the designer, and whoever builds it later.

The faction plan is in [FACTION-ROADMAP.md](design/FACTION-ROADMAP.md). It opens with 17 questions and design choices, each with a recommended answer. It's committed on `main` and merged into `server`, not pushed. I didn't deploy, since nothing in the app changed.

**The idea:** most of the expensive machinery already exists in GMAtlas: progress clocks, turns you review before they take effect, tracking where factions are, Conflict entities, and relationships. The plan adds three things on top rather than rewriting:
- **A shared faction model** that works for any game system: tier, party standing from −3 to +3, one agenda clock, Heat, an impulse, and grudges. The existing SWN engine becomes an optional deeper ruleset, with Narrative as the default and Five Leagues as a third option.
- **A Ledger behind every standing.** Each relationship score is made of reasons that fade over time, so hovering shows why ("+2 sheltered our envoy"). Helping one faction automatically costs you with its enemies, as in Blades in the Dark.
- **Tension that turns into Conflicts.** Rival factions sharing territory build tension until the system proposes a Conflict, with sides, stakes and a job each side offers the party.

**Keeping your input low:**
- New factions come from a one-tap generator, and every field is optional.
- During play, you record what the party did with one tap on a faction chip (helped, hurt, struck a deal, betrayed). It doesn't try to read meaning from your journal text.
- The off-screen faction turn runs every few scenes and at session start, as a short digest: Accept all, or ✕ or reroll a single item.

**What makes it challenging:** factions that dislike the party and are watching it can strike back, and they name what the party did. A Calm, Steady or Dire setting controls how hard it pushes.

**The questions that change the plan most:**
- **0.4:** should faction moves that don't touch the party apply without review, or should everything wait for you?
- **0.8 and 0.9:** is −3 to +3 the right scale for standing, both with the party and between factions?
- **0.13:** you just made the Advisor admin-only, so I put faction news in the Navigator, the Factions panel, the Timeline and chips on the factions present in a scene instead. Is that right?
- **0.2:** which game systems need their own faction rules first?

The work is split into phases F0–F8. F1, the faction model and Ledger, can start once section 0 is answered.

**Encoding problem on `server`:** its copy of CLAUDE.md has about 50 garbled characters (dashes showing as "â€”"). They were there before this change, so I left them alone. I can fix them in a separate commit if you want.

**The baseline**
- **What wins:** the PDF is the authority. Your answers, the app's architecture rules and the other roadmaps count only where they don't contradict it.
- **Mapping:** every service the PDF describes is matched to what GMAtlas already has, so nothing gets a second copy of the data.
- **Conflicts settled:** nine places where sources disagreed are recorded, with how each was resolved. One example: the app's "Conflict" entity type is now the Encounter, so a faction Conflict gets its own type.
- **Originality:** all names are now GMAtlas's own (Reach, Stance, Attention, Signs, Drive, Memory), so the system can be sold.
- **The PDF itself:** `requirements/` is excluded from git, so the PDF exists only on your machine. The baseline summarizes it closely enough to stand on its own.

**How your answers landed**
- **Answer 2 ("providers"):** I dropped the idea. There is one original system, plus an optional setting for how an action's outcome is rolled: GMAtlas's own roll by default, or SWN, an oracle, or the GM. This is the PDF's own "rules adapter", explained plainly.
- **Answer 9 (−3 to +3 standing):** this fits the PDF's richer relationships by splitting each one into a −3 to +3 stance, structural ties (debt, treaty, rival claim), trust and intensity. Every number can show the reasons behind it.
- **Answer 5 (when turns run):** the campaign-turn model gets a Faction Events step. Other rulesets can choose every N Encounters, a turn step, or the Faction Turn button.
- **Answer 6 (death, merger, split):** each is always reviewed, and each comes with a set of proposed consequences for its allies and its enemies.
- **Answer 12 (retaliation):** it has to be in proportion to what caused it (within one level of severity), fit the faction's methods, and vary. The same kind of response is never used twice in a row against the party, it often targets the party's allies or assets rather than the party, and it names the grudge that caused it.

**Research:** a fresh pass covered faction-design advice, faction tools (Foundry modules, browser and printable trackers), and how strategy games and productivity apps present this kind of information. Nineteen findings shaped the design, each with its source. Two things couldn't be confirmed from public sources: the faction rules in *Sundered Isles* and in *Cities Without Number*. Nothing depends on them.

**Changes across panels (U1–U15)**
- **Peek popover:** clicking any character, place or faction opens a small pinnable card with quick actions before it opens a full tab.
- **Faction Events becomes Factions,** with the PDF's eight views shown only as they become relevant.
- **Shared badges:** one style of normal/watch/review/critical badge across the nav, with an icon and a word as well as color.
- **Navigator:** the Navigator column gets a Factions card, and the turn-step list gets a Run Faction Turn button.
- **Timeline:** it becomes the single place to see campaign history in order.
- **Review Queue:** Accept, Modify, Reject, Defer, with keyboard shortcuts, and a decision card that shows only the essentials until you expand it.

I've applied these to the design only. No code has changed yet.

Nothing changes on screen yet except more relationship types in the type list; the new faction data has no editor until the Factions panel in phase F1.

**What's in it:**
- **One place for faction data.** Each campaign now has a `factions` store for objectives, projects, operations, a Ledger of standing changes, events, triggers, hooks and the faction settings. "Auto-apply quiet moves" is on by default. Existing faction events, turn counts, SWN fields and faction Threads stay where they were. The campaign format moves to version 2.
- **The Ledger starts filled.** When an older campaign is opened, each Allied With link between two factions becomes a starting +2 entry, and each Rival Of link a −2.
  - The links themselves aren't changed.
  - Rivalries involving NPCs aren't counted.
  - The entries are created only once, even if two devices open the same campaign.
  - Entries are only ever added, never edited or removed.
- **14 new relationship types.**
  - Between people and factions: Leads, Assigned To, Opposed To, Dependent On, In Debt To, Investigating, Protects, Attacked, Encountered.
  - Territory: Occupies, Claims, Contests, Influences, Blockades.
  - Owns now also accepts an Item or a Location.
  - A link that points at the wrong kind of entity is marked ⚠ for the GM and never deleted.
- **Detail on faction links.** A link can carry a standing from −3 to +3 (which can be pinned) and trust. It can also hold ties such as Patron or Treaty-bound with a condition, how much it matters, who knows about it, how firmly territory is held, and where it came from. Bad values are ignored or held to their limits.
- **Sync** carries all of it as ordinary campaign data.

**Testing:** all tests pass: 902 on `main`, 913 on `server`, plus the 43 server tests. The 7 new ones cover:
- an older export importing unchanged;
- a save-and-reload round trip keeping every record and reference;
- the Ledger being filled only once;
- nothing being removed;
- a damaged factions store being repaired;
- the new link detail.

Every simulated-browser check passes on `server`, including sign-in, sync and the two-device checks. The sign-in and sync checks can't run on `main` because that code exists only on `server`.

**One thing to watch:** a device still running a build from before this one won't sync the new factions store. It will still keep the store intact when it opens the campaign. That only matters while devices are on mixed builds.

Next is phase F1: the faction profile fields and the Factions panel, including an editor for this link detail. The four open questions at the top of the roadmap still need your answers, but none of them blocks F1.

Phase F1 is built and on dev as v0.113.7. The right-nav **Faction Events** button is now **Factions**, opening a new panel; everything the old panel had moved under its History tab. It's committed on `main` (two commits) and merged into `server`, not pushed.

**The Factions panel.** Tabs appear only once there's something to show in them:
- **Overview:** escalating Conflicts, plans in motion with their latest Sign, every faction's standing with the party, and recent events. An empty campaign gets three buttons: **Generate a starting trio**, **Generate one faction**, or **New faction**.
- **Factions:** searchable cards. Tapping one opens that faction's page.
- **Web:** a table of how each faction regards every other faction and the party; on a phone it's a list of pairs. Tap any cell to see the reasons, grouped. From there you can record a +1/−1 change or pin a value.
- **Plans:** each objective and its Signs, the visible changes the world sees as the plan advances.
- **Conflicts:** each one's place on the seven-step ladder and its sides.
- **History:** everything the old Faction Events panel had, including Step, Full Round, the roster, missions and the event log. Your jump chips and the dock-into-WHERE button now land here.

**A faction's page** has these sections in order: Identity, Motivation & Behavior, Faces, Objectives, Capabilities & State, Relationships, Territory, Memory, History, Turn controls. Highlights:
- Behavior is set with six sliders, such as Cautious ↔ Reckless, plus checkboxes for preferred methods.
- **Advance** on an objective fills its next segment and shows the matching Sign.
- Faces are added with the NPC picker, and memories through the inline prompt.

**Generating factions:**
- The tables are original, in generic, fantasy, sci-fi and cyberpunk flavors.
- Each faction comes with a face NPC to talk to and an objective that has four Signs.
- Every faction after the first wants something an existing faction wants, or claims its territory, so there's tension from the start.
- The trio also gets one extra friendly or unfriendly tie.
- The same seed always gives the same result.

**Standing** comes from the Ledger:
- Some entries fade over a few turns.
- A change ripples: a faction's close allies shift half as far in the same direction, and its enemies half as far the other way.
- A pinned value overrides everything else.
- An Allied With or Rival Of link you set by hand counts as +2 or −2.
- The app only switches a link between Linked, Allied With and Rival Of as standing changes. It never retypes a link you gave another type.

**Peek card:** tapping a faction on WHO/WHERE or in the panel opens a small card with its summary, standing, current objective, **Open**, **Pin** and **Faction page**. Those WHO/WHERE thumbnails also show standing as a symbol, a number and a word, such as "⚔ −2 Hostile", so it never relies on color alone.

**Testing:** all tests pass: 912 on `main` (10 new), 923 on `server`, plus the 43 server tests. A new simulated-browser check covers making a trio, the Web table, recording a change and pinning, editing the faction page, Advance, memories, faces, the peek card and History. It passes with no console errors, and so do all the other checks on `server`.

I went with the roadmap's recommendations on two open questions: the trio is offered, never automatic, and the classic SWN controls stay under History. The other two (the every-3-Encounters trigger and an app-wide Review Queue) matter from F3/F4.

**Still to do in F1:** peek cards on Cast, the Graph, the Timeline, the wiki and Encounter chips, and the small two-hop graph beside Relationships. Next after that is F2: events and history.

Phase F2 (events and history) is built, along with the rest of F1. It's on dev as v0.113.8, committed on `main` and merged into `server`. Nothing is pushed.

**Faction events can now be undone:**
- **Quick actions.** Tapping a faction now offers Helped, Hurt, Struck a deal, Betrayed and Exposed. Each asks you to confirm in the card first. Recording one:
  - changes how that faction regards the party;
  - nudges that faction's close allies and rivals too (half as far, allies the same way, rivals the opposite way);
  - adds a memory;
  - writes a journal entry when it's significant or major.
- **Factions › History** lists every event, newest first. Each shows its turn and the list of things it changed, with an **Undo** button and filters by faction and kind.
- **Record event** is a form in History: who acted, toward whom, the stance and trust change, a memory, how significant it is, and who knows about it.
- **Undo never erases anything.** The event stays, marked undone, and its journal entry is taken back. If you've edited something the event changed since, Undo stops and tells you instead of overwriting your edit.
- **A faction's page** shows its history of changes.

**The Timeline is now the campaign's full history:** scenes and their Encounters, journal entries, and faction events, each with its turn number. You can filter by kind, or to one faction, Location, Conflict or NPC.

**Wiki:** faction and Conflict pages gain Standing and History sections. Player View shows only public events and how the faction regards the party, never how factions regard each other.

**The rest of F1:**
- The peek card now also opens from Graph nodes, relationship chips in Cast, and the name chips on the Story Dashboard's WHO/WHERE/WHAT detail cards. Those used to open the entity directly; now it takes one more tap, on **Open** inside the card.
- A faction page shows a small graph of everything within two links, beside Relationships.
- Wiki links still go straight to the page they name, because the wiki is for reading and its portal view has no peek card.

**Testing:**
- **Tests:** all pass. There are 918 on `main` (6 new) and 929 on `server`, plus the 43 server tests.
- **Simulated-browser check:** a new one walks through the quick actions, cancel, Record event, the filters, Undo, the local graph and the Timeline filters. It passes with no console errors.
- **Other checks:** all pass except one. `default-smoke` has 6 sync-related failures, and it fails the same way on `server` before this merge, so F2 didn't cause it. I haven't looked into why.
- **Timeline check:** one check expected the old empty-Timeline wording, so I updated it.

The design for recording and undoing changes is written up in [faction-change-sets.md](design/adr/faction-change-sets.md). Next is F3: the Faction Turn, where factions act on their own and you see a preview first.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **GMAtlas rules:** one campaign document; store.js is the only persistence module; pure domain layer (clone and return a new campaign); delegated `data-*` handlers in shell.js; lossless migration; content is data.
   - **Change sets (new in F2):**
     - Each change is a slot `{kind: entity | entity-item | link | record}` with before and after values; `undefined` means absent.
     - Apply checks and writes in order on a scratch copy. On any conflict nothing is applied and the set is marked `conflicted` with both versions. `force` applies anyway and takes the current value as its before.
     - Skipping a change also skips its dependents (`dependentsOf`).
     - Undo is an inverse set marked `undoOf`.
     - The Ledger only appends. Undo adds a reversal entry (`reverses`), and `liveLedgerEntries` hides each pair from breakdowns. A migrated seed entry is not reversed.
     - `diffChanges` turns any mutator into changes.
     - Idempotency keys.
     - The journal entry for a significant or major event goes in the same change set.
   - **Build and test:**
     - Build: `node scripts/build.js`.
     - main: `npm test` gives 918 (891 domain + 27 migrate).
     - server: 929 (902 + 27); server tests (`cd server && npm test`) give 43.

It mattered only for the "future" half of your logging request. Nothing else depends on it.

**What you asked for:** every prompt and response in this session, past and future, written to a log file "as before." I couldn't find any earlier log, in the repo or in the old session transcripts, so I built a new one:

- **The script.** [.claude/logs/export_log.py](.claude/logs/export_log.py) reads Claude Code's own record of a session. It writes your prompts and my text replies to a Markdown file and skips tool calls and their output. Each run rewrites the file in full.
- **The past.** Done. This session's log is at [.claude/logs/43364e2d-f97c-4906-8f76-fb5b33358c67.md](.claude/logs/43364e2d-f97c-4906-8f76-fb5b33358c67.md).
- **The future.** That's the part you turned down. I tried to add a Stop hook to `.claude/settings.local.json`. A Stop hook is a command Claude Code runs automatically each time I finish a reply; this one would have re-run the script so the log stayed current. You declined it, then declined a smaller change to the script for the same purpose. I tested the hook command by hand and it worked, but it was never added to the settings.

**What declining it means:**
- The log won't update itself. It's correct up to when I last ran the script, and anything after that is missing until it runs again.
- Nothing else is affected: no app code, tests, commits or deploys.
- Your original request was "past and future", so without the hook the "future" part depends on me remembering to re-run the script.

**Your options:**
1. **Allow the hook.** The log updates after every reply with no effort from either of us. This is the dependable way to meet "past and future".
2. **No hook.** I re-run the script at the end of each turn. I may forget, especially after the conversation is compacted.
3. **Ask when you want it.** You say "update the log" and I run it.

One more thing: `.claude/logs/` isn't in `.gitignore`, so `git status` now shows `.claude/` as untracked. I won't commit it. If you want it hidden, I can add `.claude/logs/` to `.git/info/exclude`, a local ignore list that isn't committed.
